L1469: Source: Table 3 of Applicant's PPK Analysis Report for GDX-44-005.
L1470: Abbreviations: BMI, body mass index; eGFR, estimated glomerular filtration rate; SD, standard deviation
L1471: **Methods:** The PPK analysis was performed by NONMEM (version 7.4.3) using the first order conditional estimate method with interaction option. A two-compartment model parameterized by CL, Q, V1, and V2 was used to describe the PK data and all parameters were scaled by body weight using a power of 0.75 for CL and Q, and 1 for V1 and V2. Given that the eGFR was estimated using the Schwartz equation for children and the CKD-EPI equation for adults which could not be continuously linked, the covariate effect of eGFR on CL was characterized in different format for adult and pediatric subjects.
L1472: Q = \theta_2 \cdot (BW/70)^{0.75} \cdot \exp(\eta_2)
L1473: V_1 = \theta_3 \cdot (BW/70) \cdot \exp(\eta_3)
L1474: V_2 = \theta_4 \cdot (BW/70) \cdot \exp(\eta_4)
L1475: \text{For adults: } CL = \theta_1 \cdot (BW/70)^{0.75} \cdot (\theta_5 \cdot \exp(-\exp(-\theta_6 \cdot ((eGFR/98.5) - \theta_7))) \cdot \exp(\eta_1))
L1476: \text{For pediatrics: } CL = \theta_1 \cdot (BW/70)^{0.75} \cdot (eGFR/108)^{\theta_8} \cdot \exp(\eta_1)
L1477: **Results:** The parameter estimates are listed in Table 104. Goodness-of-fit plots for the final model are shown in Figure 14.
L1478: **Table 104. Parameter Estimates for Gadopiclenol Population PK Model**
L1479: Parameter	Estimate (%RSE)	95% CI	CV%
CL(L/h) = \theta_1(\text{Weight}/70)^{0.75}(\theta_5 \cdot \text{EXP}(-\text{EXP}(-\theta_6((\text{eGFR}/98.5) - \theta_7))) \cdot \text{EXP}(\eta_1)) Iadult	
CL(L/h) = \theta_1(\text{Weight}/70)^{0.75}(\text{eGFR}/108)^{\theta_8} \cdot \text{EXP}(\eta_1) Ichildren	
\theta_1: CL typical value	5.15 (2.3)	(4.91, 5.39)	
\theta_5: Asymptotic (maximal) effect of eGFR on adults CL	1.53 (7.3)	(1.31, 1.75)	
\theta_6: growth rate of adults CL	2.34 (10.7)	(1.85, 2.83)	
\theta_7: inflexion point of adults CL	0.523 (9.5)	(0.426, 0.62)	
\theta_8: Exponent for eGFR on children CL	0.396 (30.1)	(0.163, 0.629)	
\eta_1 (IIV CL)	0.0259 (21.4)	(0.015, 0.0368)	16.2
Q (L/h) = \theta_2(\text{Weight}/70)^{0.75} \cdot \text{EXP}(\eta_2)	
\theta_2: Q typical value	4.71 (9)	(3.88, 5.54)	
\eta_2 (IIV Q)	0.186 (34.6)	(0.06, 0.312)	45.2
V_1 (L) = \theta_3(\text{Weight}/70) \cdot \text{EXP}(\eta_3)	
\theta_3: V_1 typical value	7.47 (3.3)	(6.99, 7.95)	
\eta_3 (IIV V_1)	0.0516 (17.3)	(0.0341, 0.0691)	23.0
V_2 (L) = \theta_4(\text{Weight}/70) \cdot \text{EXP}(\eta_4)	
\theta_4: V_2 typical value	4.88 (4.5)	(4.44, 5.32)	
\eta_4 (IIV V_2)	0.18 (36.1)	(0.0528, 0.307)	44.4
Residual error	
\epsilon_1: proportional component	0.021 (12.3)	(0.0159, 0.0261)	14.6
L1480: Source: Table 5 of Applicant's PPK Analysis Report for GDX-44-005.
L1481: Abbreviations: CI, confidence interval; CV%, coefficient of variation; PK, pharmacokinetics; RSE, relative standard error
L1482: **Figure 14. Goodness-of-Fit Plots for the Final Population Pharmacokinetic Model**
L1483: Source: Figures 1,4, 9, and 10 of Section 15.4.2 of Applicant's PPK Analysis Report for GDX-44-005.
L1484: Abbreviations: CWRES, conditional weighted residuals; HV, healthy volunteer
L1485: **Reviewer's Comments on Applicant's PPK Analysis:** The Applicant's analysis for Study GDX-44-005 is generally acceptable for describing the PK of gadopiclenol in adults with normal and impaired renal functions as well as in pediatric subjects. In this NDA submission, the Applicant submitted another PPK report titled "Pharmacokinetics, safety and efficacy of a new gadolinium-based contrast agent, gadopiclenol, in pediatric patients from 2 to 17 years of age undergoing contrast-enhanced MRI, the Phase II Clinical Study (GDX-44-007)" based on dataset "gdx_pool_v3.xpt", where PPK data of 59 pediatric patients and 46 adults were included. Since the GDX-44-007 PPK report was generated previously, and all data of "gdx_pool_v3.xpt" were included for GDX-44-005 PPK analysis, no separate review for the GDX-44-007 PPK report is included.
L1486: ## 16.3.2.2. FDA Reviewer's Analysis
L1487: **Objectives:** To predict and compare the following gadopiclenol exposure metrics based on individual subject's PK parameters obtained from the Applicant's PPK analysis:
L1488: - Plasma concentration at 10, 20, and 30 min after i.v. injection of 0.05 mmol/kg dose in adult subjects with normal and impaired renal function.
- Plasma concentration at 10, 20, and 30 min in adult subjects with severely impaired renal function after i.v. injection of 0.035 mmol/kg, a 30% reduced dose to match the exposure with other renal groups.
- AUC comparison between adult subjects with different renal functions.
- Comparison of plasma concentration at 10, 20, and 30 min across different age groups after i.v. injection of 0.05 mmol/kg dose.
- Comparison of plasma concentration at 10, 20, and 30 min across different body weight groups after i.v. injection of 0.05 mmol/kg dose
L1489: **Method:** R version 4.1.0 was used for calculation and plotting.
L1490: **Results:** The predicted concentration results are shown in Figure 15 to Figure 18 and Table 105 and Table 106.
L1491: **Figure 15. Gadopiclenol Plasma Concentration at 10, 20, and 30 min After Intravenous Injection of 0.05 mmol/kg Dose in Subjects With Normal and Impaired Renal Function**
L1492: Source: Reviewer's Analysis Based on Applicant's Final PPK Model for Study GDX-44-005.
L1493: **Table 105. Gadopiclenol Concentrations in Impaired Renal Function Relative to Normal Renal Function at 10, 20 and 30 min After Intravenous Injection of 0.05 mmol/kg Dose**
L1494: Renal Function	Concentration at
10 min	20 min	30 min
Mild renal impairment	↑13%	↑21%	↑27%
Moderate renal impairment	↑12%	↑18%	↑24%
Severe renal impairment	↑33%	↑41%	↑52%
L1495: Source: Reviewer's Analysis Based on Applicant's Final PPK Model for Study GDX-44-005.
L1496: Due to higher gadopiclenol concentration at 10 to 30 min and about 9 times higher AUC in subjects with severe renal impairment compared to subjects with normal renal function, a potential dose reduction was evaluated through PK simulation for subjects with severe renal impairment. With 30% dose reduction in subjects with severe renal impairment, the predicted concentrations are shown in Figure 16 and Table 106. Although the predicted concentrations during the time window of 10 to 30 min in severe renal impairment subjects are closer to other groups, the AUC would still be 7 times higher than subjects with normal renal function.

L1516: Figure 8 displays two graphs showing the relationship of Gadopiclenol Dose to Cmax and AUC. The left graph plots Cmax (µg/mL) versus P03277 administered dose (mmol/kg), showing a dose-proportional increase. The right graph plots AUCinf (µg/mL*h) versus P03277 administered dose (mmol/kg), also showing a dose-proportional increase.
L1517: Figure 9 displays three box plots showing Gadopiclenol Plasma Concentration (mg/L) versus Age (years) for patients with normal renal function, measured at 10 min, 20 min, and 30 min post-injection of a 0.05 mmol/kg dose. The age groups are 2-5, 6-11, 12-17, and 18-60 years. Concentration generally increases with age across all time points. At 10 min, concentrations range from approximately 250 mg/L to 500 mg/L. At 20 min, concentrations range from approximately 250 mg/L to 400 mg/L. At 30 min, concentrations range from approximately 200 mg/L to 350 mg/L.
L1518: Figure 10 displays three box plots showing Gadopiclenol Plasma Concentration (mg/L) at 10 min, 20 min, and 30 min after intravenous injection of a 0.05 mmol/kg dose, comparing subjects with Normal, Mild, Moderate, and Severe renal impairment (n=8 per group). Concentration generally decreases over time and increases with worsening renal function. At 10 min, Normal subjects show concentrations around 350 mg/L, while Severe subjects show concentrations around 450 mg/L. At 30 min, Normal subjects show concentrations around 250 mg/L, while Severe subjects show concentrations around 350 mg/L.
L1519: Figure 11 displays three box plots showing simulated Gadopiclenol Plasma Concentration (mg/L) at 10 min, 20 min, and 30 min after intravenous injection. The plots compare four groups: Normal (n=8), Mild (n=8), Moderate (n=8), and Severe (n=8) renal function. The Y-axis ranges from 200 to 400 mg/L. At 10 min, Normal concentration is highest (median ~350 mg/L), followed by Severe (~300 mg/L), Moderate (~280 mg/L), and Mild (~250 mg/L). At 20 min and 30 min, the concentration decreases for all groups, with Normal remaining the highest, and Severe remaining the lowest. The concentration generally decreases over time for all groups.
L1520: Flowchart illustrating the GDX-44-010 Study Design.
The study begins with Screening, leading to Randomization. Randomization splits participants into two groups:
Group 1: gadopiclenol 0.05 mmol/kg
Group 2: gadobutrol 0.1 mmol/kg
Both groups proceed to a Safety Follow-up period. Following the Safety Follow-up, Group 1 receives gadobutrol 0.1 mmol/kg, and Group 2 receives gadopiclenol 0.05 mmol/kg. Both groups then proceed to a second Safety Follow-up period.
Below the main flow, the study visits (V) are detailed:
V1 (Visit 1) occurs 0 to 7 days after Screening.
V2 (1st MRI) occurs 1 day after V1.
V3 occurs 1 day after V2.
V4 (2nd MRI) occurs 1 day after V3.
V5 occurs 1 day after V4.
The time interval between V2 and V3 is specified as min: 2 days / max: 14 days.
Legend: V: visit
L1521: Scatter plot showing the incidence of Gadopiclenol-Treatment Emergent Adverse Events (AEs) by dose in the Pooled Safety Set (n=1047). The X-axis represents Gadopiclenol Dose (mmol/kg) ranging from 0 to 0.35. The Y-axis represents Patients with at Least One AE (%) ranging from 0 to 70. Data points are shown for doses 0.05, 0.1, 0.2, and 0.3 mmol/kg, along with 95% confidence intervals. The incidence of AEs generally increases with dose, reaching approximately 47% at 0.3 mmol/kg.
L1522: Figure 14 displays four panels of Goodness-of-Fit (GoF) plots for the Final Population Pharmacokinetic Model, stratified by renal impairment status (Healthy Volunteer (HV), mild, moderate, and severe).
The top row shows concentration versus prediction plots. The bottom row shows conditional weighted residuals (CWRES) versus prediction or time plots.
Legend:
Linear Regression (Solid Blue Line)
Loess, smooth=0.8 (Dashed Blue Line)
Renal Impairment categories are represented by different colors/shapes: HV (Healthy Volunteer), mild, moderate, and severe.
Top Left Panel: Concentrations (mg/L) vs Individual predictions (mg/L)
This plot compares observed concentrations against individual model predictions. The data points generally follow the linear regression line, indicating a good fit for the concentration-prediction relationship.
Top Right Panel: Concentrations (mg/L) vs Population predictions (mg/L)
This plot compares observed concentrations against population model predictions. The data points generally follow the linear regression line, indicating a good fit for the concentration-prediction relationship.
Bottom Left Panel: CWRES vs Population predictions (mg/L)
This plot shows the conditional weighted residuals (CWRES) versus population predictions. The residuals are scattered randomly around the zero line, with most points falling between the dashed horizontal lines at approximately -2.5 and 2.5, suggesting that the model adequately captures the variability in the data.
Bottom Right Panel: CWRES vs Time (hours)
This plot shows the conditional weighted residuals (CWRES) versus time. The residuals are scattered randomly around the zero line, with most points falling between the dashed horizontal lines at approximately -2.5 and 2.5, suggesting that the model adequately captures the variability in the data over time.
L1523: Figure 15 displays box plots illustrating Gadopiclenol Plasma Concentration (mg/L) at 10, 20, and 30 minutes after intravenous injection of a 0.05 mmol/kg dose, comparing subjects with Normal, Mild, Moderate, and Severe renal function impairment. The sample size (n=8) is indicated for each renal function group.
The Y-axis represents Concentration (mg/L), ranging from 200 to 500.
At 10 min: Concentration increases significantly across renal function groups. Normal (approx. 350 mg/L) < Mild (approx. 380 mg/L) < Moderate (approx. 400 mg/L) < Severe (approx. 450 mg/L). The Severe group shows the highest median concentration and the largest interquartile range (IQR).
At 20 min: Concentration decreases compared to 10 min. Normal (approx. 300 mg/L) < Mild (approx. 330 mg/L) < Moderate (approx. 350 mg/L) < Severe (approx. 380 mg/L). The Severe group maintains the highest concentration.
At 30 min: Concentration continues to decrease. Normal (approx. 280 mg/L) < Mild (approx. 310 mg/L) < Moderate (approx. 330 mg/L) < Severe (approx. 360 mg/L). The Severe group shows the highest concentration, although the overall range is narrower than at 10 min.
In all time points, subjects with Severe renal function impairment exhibit the highest plasma concentrations, while Normal renal function subjects show the lowest concentrations.
L1524: Figure 16 displays three box plots showing Gadopiclenol Plasma Concentration (mg/L) versus Renal Function (Normal, Mild, Moderate, Severe) at 10 min, 20 min, and 30 min. The Y-axis ranges from 200 to 400 mg/L. The X-axis groups are Normal (n=8), Mild (n=8), Moderate (n=8), and Severe (n=8). At 10 min, concentrations for Normal, Mild, and Moderate groups are clustered around 300-350 mg/L, while the Severe group is significantly higher, around 380-400 mg/L. At 20 min, concentrations for Normal, Mild, and Moderate groups are clustered around 300-350 mg/L, while the Severe group is significantly higher, around 380-400 mg/L. At 30 min, concentrations for Normal, Mild, and Moderate groups are clustered around 300-350 mg/L, while the Severe group is significantly higher, around 380-400 mg/L. The plots illustrate that concentrations are highest in the Severe renal impairment group across all time points.
L1525: Figure 17 displays three box plots showing Gadopiclenol Plasma Concentration (mg/L) versus Age (years) for patients with Normal Renal Function after intravenous injection of 0.05 mmol/kg dose. The plots are shown at 10 min, 20 min, and 30 min. The Y-axis (Concentration) ranges from 200 to 500 mg/L. The X-axis (Age) is divided into four groups: 2-5, 6-11, 12-17, and 18-60 years. The concentration generally increases with age across all time points. At 10 min, the 18-60 age group shows the highest median concentration (around 400 mg/L), while the 2-5 age group shows the lowest (around 320 mg/L). Similar trends are observed at 20 min and 30 min, with the 18-60 age group consistently showing the highest concentration.
L1526: Figure 18 displays box plots illustrating Gadopiclenol Plasma Concentration (mg/L) versus Body Weight (kg) groups (10.1-25, 25.1-50, 50.1-75, 75.1-100.2) at three time points: 10 min, 20 min, and 30 min. The concentration generally increases over time across all body weight groups.
At 10 min: Concentration ranges from approximately 200 mg/L to 500 mg/L. The highest median concentration (around 380 mg/L) is observed in the 75.1-100.2 kg group. The lowest median concentration (around 320 mg/L) is observed in the 10.1-25 kg group.
At 20 min: Concentration ranges from approximately 200 mg/L to 400 mg/L. The highest median concentration (around 350 mg/L) is observed in the 75.1-100.2 kg group. The lowest median concentration (around 280 mg/L) is observed in the 10.1-25 kg group.
At 30 min: Concentration ranges from approximately 200 mg/L to 350 mg/L. The highest median concentration (around 320 mg/L) is observed in the 75.1-100.2 kg group. The lowest median concentration (around 220 mg/L) is observed in the 10.1-25 kg group.
The plots show that higher body weight groups generally exhibit higher plasma concentrations of Gadopiclenol at all measured time points.
